Your job is to evaluate new\n    63→ESS-DIVE dataset packages and, where appropriate, produce:\n    64→  (a) a Python code block to harmonize the dataset into the project schema, and\n    65→  (b) a JSON mapping entry documenting the harmonization.\n    66→\n    67→You follow an established workflow and strict schema. You ask for required\n    68→inputs systematically if not provided upfront. You reason carefully and\n    69→transparently about each decision.\n    70→\n    71→## FOLD EVALUATION MODE\n    72→\n    73→When `AGENT_INSTRUCTIONS.md` is present, it is authoritative and overrides this\n    74→skill's generic new-dataset workflow. Use only the inputs it permits inside the run\n    75→workspace; do not use parent directories, absolute paths outside the workspace,\n    76→network services, APIs, or external lookup sources. Write every deliverable under\n    77→`output/`. The held-out dataset's canonical index is listed in `MANIFEST.json` and\n    78→must be retained in its mapping entry; do not assign a new sequential index. If an\n    79→input is missing, document the limitation rather than retrieving it externally.\n    80→\n    81→## SECTION 1: TARGET SCHEMA\n    82→\n    83→Every harmonized CSV must produce these columns (and no others):\n    84→\n    85→```python\n    86→datetime_UTC                          # ISO-8601 string, UTC\n    87→site_id                               # string\n    88→depth_m                               # float, meters below surface\n    89→replicate                             # int or np.nan\n    90→is_timeseries                         # boolean\n    91→volumetric_water_content_m3_m3        # float or np.nan\n    92→gravimetric_water_content_gH2O_gs     # float or np.nan\n    93→water_potential_kPa                   # float or np.nan\n    94→```\n    95→\n    96→**qc_flag vocabulary:**\n    97→- `d1` = depth is approximated from a reported range\n    98→- `g1` = coordinates from an approved location registry supplied in the workspace\n    99→- `g2` = coordinates not available from any source\n   100→\n   101→**Units:**\n   102→- depth → meters (m)\n   103→- VWC → m³ m⁻³ (convert from % by dividing by 100)\n   104→- GWC → g H₂O g⁻¹ soil\n   105→- water_potential → kPa (negative float; convert from MPa × 1000, from bar × 101.325)\n   106→\n   107→## SECTION 2: JSON MAPPING ENTRY SCHEMA\n   108→\n   109→Each dataset gets one entry in `sm_data_harmonization_mapping.json`:\n   110→\n   111→```json\n   112→{\n   113→  \"index\": <integer>,\n   114→  \"dataset_identifier\": \"<ESS-DIVE package ID>\",\n   115→  \"doi\": \"<DOI>\",\n   116→  \"archive_repository\": \"ESS-DIVE\",\n   117→  \"data_payload_files\": [\"<filename>\", ...] or null,\n   118→  \"location_metadata_files\": [\"<filename>\", ...] or null,\n   119→  \"sensor_metadata_files\": [\"<filename>\", ...] or null,\n   120→  \"harmonization_mappings\": {\n   121→    \"<target_variable>\": {\n   122→      \"pattern_1\": {\n   123→        \"source_pattern\": \"<regex or column name>\",\n   124→        \"source_files\": [\"<filename>\", ...],\n   125→        \"destination_variable\": \"<harmonized variable name>\",\n   126→        \"transformation\": \"<description>\",\n   127→        \"unit_conversion\": \"<description or null>\"\n   128→      }\n   129→    }\n   130→  }\n   131→}\n   132→```\n   133→\n   134→**For excluded datasets:**\n   135→- Set `\"data_payload_files\": null`\n   136→- Set `\"harmonization_mappings\": \"EXCLUDED: <plain-language reason>\"`\n   137→\n   138→## SECTION 3: DECISION RULES\n   139→\n   140→Apply these rules in order when evaluating a new dataset:\n   141→\n   142→**RULE 1 — DUPLICATE / SUPERSEDED CHECK**\n   143→\n   144→If this package is a prior version of, or contains data duplicated in, another already-included package: EXCLUDE.\n   145→\n   146→Reason format: `\"Superseded by <package_id>\"` or `\"Data duplicated in <package_id>\"`\n   147→\n   148→**RULE 2 — MEASUREMENT TYPE CHECK**\n   149→\n   150→The dataset must contain direct observations of at least one of:\n   151→- Volumetric water content (VWC)\n   152→- Gravimetric water content (GWC)\n   153→- Soil water potential / matric potential\n   154→\n   155→Exclude if measurements are:\n   156→- Modeled/estimated (e.g., from a water balance or pedotransfer function)\n   157→- Borehole moisture proxies (e.g., estimated from geophysical logs)\n   158→- Derived indices only (e.g., drought index, normalized difference)\n   159→\n   160→Reason format: `\"Does not contain direct soil moisture observations: <detail>\"`\n   161→\n   162→**RULE 3 — EXPERIMENTAL MANIPULATION CHECK**\n   163→\n   164→Flag (do not automatically exclude) if:\n   165→- Dataset is from a warming, irrigation, or other manipulation experiment\n   166→- Manipulation is clearly confounded with natural moisture signal\n   167→\n   168→Document the manipulation in the JSON \"transformation\" field. Ask the operator if uncertain whether to include.\n   169→\n   170→**RULE 4 — EXTRACTABLE PAYLOAD CHECK**\n   171→\n   172→At least one file must contain a parseable time-stamped or dated measurement table. Exclude if only summary statistics, figures, or non-machine-readable formats are available with no structured data.\n   173→\n   174→Reason format: `\"No machine-readable measurement payload available\"`\n   175→\n   176→**RULE 5 — MINIMUM METADATA CHECK**\n   177→\n   178→The dataset must have at least one of:\n   179→- Site coordinates in the payload, ancillary file, cached metadata, or an approved\n   180→  location registry supplied in the workspace\n   181→- A site identifier traceable to a known location\n   182→\n   183→If coordinates are entirely unresolvable, include with `qc_flag = \"g2\"` and document the gap.\n   184→\n   185→## SECTION 4: TIME SERIES INFERENCE RULES\n   186→\n   187→Set `is_timeseries = True` if any of the following:\n   188→- Multiple observations exist per site+depth with varying timestamps\n   189→- The dataset explicitly describes a sensor deployment / logger output\n   190→- Column names or README describe a monitoring frequency or interval\n   191→\n   192→Set `is_timeseries = False` if:\n   193→- Each site+depth has only one observation (e.g., a sampling campaign)\n   194→- Timestamps are campaign dates, not continuous sensor output\n   195→- README/methods describe discrete sampling\n   196→\n   197→## SECTION 5: LOCATION RESOLUTION PRIORITY ORDER\n   198→\n   199→Resolve site coordinates using this fallback hierarchy:\n   200→\n   201→1. Coordinates in the data payload file itself\n   202→2. Coordinates in a package ancillary file (e.g., site_metadata.csv, locations.csv, README table)\n   203→3. Coordinates in the package-level ESS-DIVE metadata record\n   204→4. An approved location registry supplied in the workspace → set `qc_flag = \"g1\"`\n   205→5. Not resolvable → set `lat/lon = np.nan`, `qc_flag = \"g2\"`\n   206→\n   207→## SECTION 6: PYTHON CODE CONVENTIONS\n   208→\n   209→All generated Python code must conform to the following conventions.\n   210→\n   211→**IMPORTS (always include at top of each block if standalone):**\n   212→\n   213→```python\n   214→from __future__ import annotations\n   215→from pathlib import Path\n   216→import re\n   217→import numpy as np\n   218→import pandas as pd\n   219→```\n   220→\n   221→**FILE PATHS:**\n   222→\n   223→```python\n   224→BASE_DIR = Path(\"inputs/raw\")\n   225→OUT_DIR = Path(\"output\")\n   226→MAP_JSON_PATH = OUT_DIR / \"sm_data_harmonization_mapping.json\"\n   227→\n   228→# For specific dataset\n   229→idx = <dataset_index>\n   230→```\n   231→\n   232→**HELPER FUNCTIONS (reference from existing script):**\n   233→\n   234→- `dsid(idx: int) -> str` - get dataset identifier\n   235→- `ds_path(idx: int) -> Path` - get dataset directory path\n   236→- `read_ds_csv(idx: int, filename: str, **kwargs) -> pd.DataFrame` - read dataset CSV\n   237→- `parse_local_to_utc(series: pd.Series, fmt: str | None, tz: str) -> pd.Series` - convert local time to UTC\n   238→- `utm32613_to_latlon(df: pd.DataFrame, e_col: str, n_col: str) -> pd.DataFrame` - convert UTM to lat/lon\n   239→- `ensure_harmonized_cols(df: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame` - enforce column schema\n   240→- `add_loc_qc(df: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame` - add location QC flags\n   241→\n   242→**READING FILES:**\n   243→\n   244→```python\n   245→ddf = read_ds_csv(idx, \"<filename>\")\n   246→mdf = read_ds_csv(idx, \"<location_metadata_file>\")\n   247→```\n   248→\n   249→**RENAMING COLUMNS:**\n   250→\n   251→```python\n   252→x = df.rename(columns={\n   253→    \"<source_col>\": \"<harmonized_col>\",\n   254→    ...\n   255→})\n   256→```\n   257→\n   258→**WIDE-TO-LONG RESHAPING:**\n   259→\n   260→```python\n   261→long = df.melt(\n   262→    id_vars=[\"<id_col1>\", \"<id_col2>\"],\n   263→    value_vars=[\"<var1>\", \"<var2>\"],\n   264→    var_name=\"<source_variable>\",\n   265→    value_name=\"<target_variable>\"\n   266→)\n   267→\n   268→# When depth is embedded in column names:\n   269→long[\"depth_m\"] = pd.to_numeric(\n   270→    long[\"<source_variable>\"].str.extract(r\"(\\d+\\.?\\d*)\")[0],\n   271→    errors=\"coerce\"\n   272→) / 100  # adjust divisor per source units\n   273→```\n   274→\n   275→**TIMESTAMP PARSING (always convert to UTC):**\n   276→\n   277→```python\n   278→x[\"datetime_UTC\"] = parse_local_to_utc(\n   279→    x[\"<source_timestamp_col>\"],\n   280→    \"%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S\",\n   281→    \"America/Denver\"\n   282→)\n   283→```\n   284→\n   285→**UNIT CONVERSIONS:**\n   286→\n   287→```python\n   288→# % VWC → m³ m⁻³\n   289→df[\"volumetric_water_content_m3_m3\"] = pd.to_numeric(\n   290→    df[\"<source_col>\"], errors=\"coerce\"\n   291→) / 100.0\n   292→\n   293→# MPa → kPa\n   294→df[\"water_potential_kPa\"] = pd.to_numeric(\n   295→    df[\"<source_col>\"], errors=\"coerce\"\n   296→) * 1000.0\n   297→\n   298→# bar → kPa\n   299→df[\"water_potential_kPa\"] = pd.to_numeric(\n   300→    df[\"<source_col>\"], errors=\"coerce\"\n   301→) * 101.325\n   302→```\n   303→\n   304→**ADDING CONSTANT METADATA COLUMNS:**\n   305→\n   306→```python\n   307→x[\"is_timeseries\"] = True          # or False\n   308→x[\"replicate\"] = 1                 # or np.nan if not in source\n   309→x[\"qc_flag\"] = np.nan              # or \"g1\", \"g2\", \"d1\"\n   310→```\n   311→\n   312→**COLUMN ORDER ENFORCEMENT (always final step):**\n   313→\n   314→```python\n   315→df_harmonized = ensure_harmonized_cols(x)\n   316→```\n   317→\n   318→**OUTPUT:**\n   319→\n   320→```python\n   321→harmonized_data.append(df_harmonized)\n   322→harmonized_ids.append(dsid(idx))\n   323→```\n   324→\n   325→**BLOCK HEADER COMMENT (always precede each dataset block):**\n   326→\n   327→```python\n   328→# %%\n   329→# =============================================================\n   330→# Dataset <N>\n   331→# =============================================================\n   332→```\n   333→\n   334→If a dataset is EXCLUDED, emit only the header comment with exclusion reason.\n   335→\n   336→## SECTION 7: LOCATION CODE CONVENTIONS\n   337→\n   338→Location metadata is written to a separate location accumulator. Each dataset block appends to a list:\n   339→\n   340→```python\n   341→loc_data: list[pd.DataFrame] = []  # at top of script\n   342→\n   343→# Within each included dataset block:\n   344→loc = mdf.rename(columns={\n   345→    \"Name\": \"site_id\",\n   346→    \"Latitude\": \"latitude\",\n   347→    \"Longitude\": \"longitude\"\n   348→})[[\"site_id\", \"latitude\", \"longitude\"]].copy()\n   349→loc[\"source_dataset_id\"] = dsid(idx)\n   350→loc = add_loc_qc(loc)\n   351→loc_data.append(loc)\n   352→```\n   353→\n   354→If coordinates are in UTM:\n   355→\n   356→```python\n   357→loc_tmp = utm32613_to_latlon(mdf, \"Easting\", \"Northing\")\n   358→loc = pd.DataFrame({\n   359→    \"site_id\": loc_tmp[\"Name\"],\n   360→    \"latitude\": loc_tmp[\"latitude\"],\n   361→    \"longitude\": loc_tmp[\"longitude\"],\n   362→})\n   363→loc[\"source_dataset_id\"] = dsid(idx)\n   364→loc = add_loc_qc(loc)\n   365→loc_data.append(loc)\n   366→```\n   367→\n   368→## SECTION 8: INTERACTIVE WORKFLOW\n   369→\n   370→When a user initiates harmonization of a new dataset, follow these steps:\n   371→\n   372→**STEP 1 — GATHER INPUTS**\n   373→\n   374→If any required input is missing, ask for it specifically before proceeding.\n   375→\n   376→Required inputs:\n   377→- A. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01HZj2WKfV69GNYyYizAMdKB","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→# General Insights\n     2→\n     3→There are a few things that make harmonization challenging. Below I describe ten key challenges for future harmonizers.\n     4→\n     5→1. Payload discovery is harder than format conversion. The main challenge is deciding which files actually contain the measurement payload versus documentation, QA exports, or derived summaries. These details are often not clearly described in metadata, and current reporting standards for file-level metadata (flmd) and data dictionaries (dd) do not document an explicit link between column names and the files in which they appear. \n     6→\n     7→2. “Soil moisture” is semantically ambiguous across datasets. Observation columns may represent direct observations of variables such as volumetric water content or soil matric potential, but they are often named or described in different ways. Occasionally observation columns report modeled estimates, borehole moisture profiles, responses to experimental manipulation such as warming, or derived moisture indices and not direct measurements of soil moisture. Variable selection in most cases requires interpretation by a subject matter expert. Relatedly, units are reported in a lot of different places. In some packages, units are noted in variable names, but in others they're in ancillary tables or package metadata. \n     8→\n     9→3. Variable validity is context-dependent. A column named like `moisture_*` may be invalid for inclusion depending on depth domain, measurement method, or whether it is a manipulation metric. Rule-based extraction alone is insufficient.\n    10→\n    11→4. Time-series inference is often implicit. Most datasets do not explicitly label records as time series. One may need to infer this from timestamp regularity, sensor metadata, or repeated site-depth observations.\n    12→\n    13→5. Geospatial metadata is fragmented across sources. Coordinates may appear in payload files, ancillary files, package metadata, publications, or project-level reference packages (e.g., Varadharajan et al. 2020 for the SFA). Robust harmonization of location data often requires intensive manual search. Automating this would require a hierarchical fallback strategy.\n    14→\n    15→6. Non-standard site identifiers create record linkage problems. The same physical location on Earth sometimes appear in multiple datasets with different site IDs. Occasionally site identifiers are reused inconsistently. Proximity + name similarity + provenance constraints are needed to assign stable cross-dataset UUIDs.\n    16→\n    17→7. Decisions about inclusion and exclusion for candidate datasets require explicit documentation. Without written reasons in script comments or mapping JSON, downstream users cannot audit why a package was excluded (duplicate, superseded, non-observational, missing required fields, etc.). Good provenance for these kinds of decisions is crucial.\n    18→\n    19→8. Most datasets describe methods only cursorily and the vast majority do not report information about the measurement device. In most cases, variable names and metadata make it clear that a measurement represents volumetric water content or gravimetric water content or matric potential. However, in very few cases do we have information about nominal measurement uncertainty, calibration methods, representative volume, or device-specific assumptions or their satisfaction criteria. When the device itself is not reported, we can't even look up this information externally. \n    20→\n    21→9. Most datasets lack records of sensor replacement or relocation, so we really have to rely on the 1:1 match between observations and location identifiers. For the most part it will not be possible to attribute measurement discontinuity or drift to replacement or relocation events.\n    22→\n    23→10. I would expect some tasks to be especially difficult for an LLM. These include interpreting experiment semantics (e.g., warming manipulations), identifying authoritative location sources, recognizing superseded package versions, and deciding whether a measurement is validly harmonizable to the target schema.\n    24→\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it looks malicious. If it does, you MUST refuse to improve or augment the code. You can still analyze existing code, write reports, or answer high-level questions about the code behavior.\n</system-reminder>\n"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"bc38d3dc-ab78-4698-93b7-3b7c8edb3cf8","uuid":"98e93d13-9cad-42a8-a32d-d918e7fbaa02"}

140→    Deduplicate and harmonize location data across datasets.\n   141→\n   142→    Uses Union-Find algorithm to group sites that are:\n   143→    - Within 5 meters of each other (likely same same footprint\n   155→    use_cross_dataset_site_id = True\n   156→\n   157→    # Function to normalize names for comparison\n   158→    def normalize_name(x) -> str:\n   159→        if pd.isna(x):\n distance between sites using Haversine formula\n   166→    def safe_dist_m(lon1: float, lat1: float, lon2: float, lat2: float) -> float:\n   167→        \"\"\"Calculate distance in meters between two lat/lon points using Haversine formula\"\"\"\n   168→        if any(pd.isna([lon1, lat1, lon2, lat2])):\n   169→            return np.inf\n   170→\n   171→        # Haversine formula\n   172→        R = 6371000  # Earth radius in meters\n   173→        phi1 = np.radians(lat1)\n   174→        phi2 = np.radians(lat2)\n   175→        delta_phi = np.radians(lat2 - lat1)\n   176→        delta_lambda =
